- [2026-08-18 Tue 10:47]--[2026-08-18 Tue 11:24] suggested span, accepted at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-18 Tue 11:27]--[2026-08-18 Tue 11:44] =>  0:17
- [2026-08-18 Tue 11:27]--[2026-08-18 Tue 11:44] assigned at review
- State "REVIEW"     from "DOING"      [2026-08-18 Tue 11:43] \\
  2(a)(b)(c) all shipped, handed back for judgement
- State "DONE"       from "REVIEW"     [2026-08-19 Wed 17:48] \\
  both review decisions made, mechanisms compose
:END:

*Where this ended up.* Two mechanisms were writing clock time by
different rules, and the intended one counted your idle time between
turns as work. Three changes fixed it: *2(a)* made clustering
kind-aware so a long turn survives (=a931eb1=); *2(b)* made apply
write one CLOCK line per run of work rather than one per span,
removing a ~45% overcount (=e297bbd=); *2(c)* silenced the
pre-cutover auto-clock-in trigger, leaving one mechanism (=c9fc1a3=).
The accumulated record was recomputed separately under
[[id:507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e][507754ba]].

*Two decisions are yours, and are the only reason this is REVIEW
rather than DONE.* Is a 5-16 line drawer readable, at
=claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor= 120s? Raising it to 300s reads
better and re-absorbs about 8 h of idle. And do you want =C-c C-t= to
start a clock again (=claude-code-ide-org-auto-clock-in-on-doing= ->
t)? Both are one-line changes either way.

Everything below is the original diagnosis followed by three shipping
journals, in the order they were written.

=38b92521='s :LOGBOOK: claimed *3:59* for a session that ran 15:28 to
18:50. Corrected to 2:44 by hand on 2026-08-17. The overcount came from
two mechanisms writing CLOCK lines into the same drawer with no knowledge
of each other.

*Mechanism 1, the intended one.* The review pass turns pause/resume
guideposts into spans and writes them on apply. Six such spans were in
that drawer, totalling 1:07, each annotated "assigned at review".

*Mechanism 2, which should not exist post-cutover.*
=claude-code-ide-org--trigger-auto-clock-in= opened a live clock when the
user set PLANNING by hand with =C-c C-t=, and it ran *continuously* until
closed — one 2:52 interval straight through every pause, overlapping
almost all of mechanism 1's spans. This is precisely the pre-cutover
behaviour the refactor removed from the hooks: =session-pause= and
=session-resume= no longer clock, so nothing stops a live clock any more.
The trigger reintroduces it by a path nobody intended.

*The third finding, and the most serious: the guidepost model is blind to
long turns.* Guideposts mark *turn boundaries*. A single long turn emits
none, so =--aggregate-guideposts= sees one lone timestamp and produces a
zero-width span. Measured on this heading: 15:28→16:37 was one 69-minute
turn (exploration, planning, writing the plan) and 16:49→17:17 another of
28 minutes. Both are real work. Both were invisible to the review pass,
which is why they had to be added by hand. Together they are 1:37 — more
than the 1:07 the review pass did record.

That reframes [[id:31f766ab-90ed-4950-bc5b-787528c8fe84][31f766ab]]. Its zero-length spans are not merely
annoying: each one is the review buffer's way of reporting *a long turn it
cannot measure*. The aggregator's docstring calls a lone timestamp
"honest — one interaction point is not evidence of a duration", which is
true of the evidence and false of the world.

*Directions, since decided* — recorded as open on the morning of
2026-08-18 and settled by that evening. The first shipped as 2(c)
below. The second was never taken. The third is *not* moot, though
this paragraph said so for a day: 2(b) makes the written lines
shorter, not non-overlapping, so a human's own CLOCK line can still
contain several of them. It is the enabling fix for accepting
hand-set transitions gracefully — see
[[id:dadc08cf-9e12-4e93-bde8-68c241622b0f][dadc08cf]]:

- Exempt hand-set PLANNING/DOING from the auto-clock-in trigger, or have
  the trigger queue a =clock_in= event instead of opening a live clock, so
  there is only one mechanism.
- Emit a guidepost on some interval *within* a turn, not only at its
  boundaries, so long turns stop being invisible. Changes what a guidepost
  means.
- Have apply refuse to write a span that overlaps an existing CLOCK line
  on the same heading. Catches the symptom wherever it comes from.

*Related but distinct:* [[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] shipped the container
exemption for this same trigger, so the trigger is already known to need
scoping. And [[id:d39f5f0d-bee3-4aa4-8494-0bddd07e9067][d39f5f0d]] says CLAUDE.md's "Session tracking"
section describes a clocking mechanism that no longer exists. This
paragraph then said the mechanism *did* still exist, which was true
until 2(c) silenced it that same evening. So CLAUDE.md is wrong about
it for a third distinct reason now: the mechanism exists, and is off
by default.

-----

*The mechanism, demonstrated 2026-08-17 rather than argued.* A long turn
does *not* emit zero guideposts — it emits exactly two, its own
boundaries. =Stop= fires =pause= when a turn ends, =UserPromptSubmit=
fires =resume= when the next begins, and nothing fires in between. So a
turn of duration D contributes two timestamps D apart, and the only thing
that decides their fate is D against the 1200-second threshold:

: SHORT turn (10 min):  [10:00]--[10:10]  =>  10 min
: LONG  turn (69 min):  [10:00]--[10:00]  =>   0 min
:                       [11:09]--[11:09]  =>   0 min

Identical evidence, opposite outcome. Worse, in the real 15:28 case the
long turn's closing =pause= was absorbed into the *following* cluster,
where it described a 3-minute turn it had nothing to do with — so the
duration is not merely lost, part of its evidence is re-attributed.

*Root cause: =--aggregate-guideposts= never reads =:kind:=.* It maps
=:ts= over the events and clusters an undifferentiated stream of
timestamps. But the two gap types mean opposite things — =pause= ->
=resume= is nobody being there, =resume= -> =pause= is one turn running.
The threshold was tuned for the first (bimodal latency over four days,
:ID: 96a51c2f). Applied to the second it inverts: the strongest evidence
of continuous work reads as absence. *The fix is therefore not retuning
1200* — raising it swallows real idle, lowering it fragments more turns.
The distinction is already on every event and is discarded before
clustering.

*Measured across the whole post-cutover record, and it corrects the
direction stated above.* 834 guideposts, 2026-08-11 onward:

| method                                  | total   |
|-----------------------------------------+---------|
| kind-blind clustering (what runs today) | 39.10 h |
| =resume= -> =pause= pairing             | 21.59 h |
| difference                              | −17.50 h |

*The net bias is overcounting, not undercounting* — about 45%. Clustering
absorbs every idle gap under 20 minutes into the surrounding span, and
CLAUDE.md defines a CLOCK entry as active work only. The long-turn
blindness is real but minor beside it: *six* turns in the entire
post-cutover history exceeded the threshold, 4.88 h in total. Two opposite
biases, and the smaller one is the one that gets noticed because it
produces a visible zero-width item.

*A post-cutover regression, specifically.* Before 2026-08-11 the hooks
clocked directly — the clock ran *through* a turn and stopped at its
boundary, which is method B. The refactor replaced a mechanism that
handled long turns correctly with one that cannot see them.

*On correcting the historical record: measure, do not rewrite.* Raw
=pause=/=resume= events survive from 2026-08-07 (910 of them, live and
archived queue files). But 31 of DONE.org's 114 CLOCK lines predate the
queue and are unrecoverable; pre-cutover lines are not affected anyway;
and every post-cutover span carries "suggested span, accepted at review"
or "assigned at review" — a human looked and said yes. Silently replacing
those with recomputed values is what
[[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]] was filed against,
where rounding destroyed a reviewed interval. The archive should keep a
documented bias rather than acquire a silent recomputation.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/elegant-questing-lamport.md][Plan]] — the epic's plan document, which
carries this child's design rather than a file of its own.

*Step 2(b) shipped 2026-08-18* (=e297bbd=): apply writes one CLOCK line
per run of work rather than one across the span.
=claude-code-ide-org--span-work-runs= is the pure decision layer, and
=claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor= (new =defcustom=, 120 s) is the
only value that now decides which idle is recorded.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =bin/test= 305/305, with each new test
checked against a mutation of the thing it pins -- floor =<== instead of
=<=, the rendered-zero filter removed, any adjacency counted as work, the
=:suggested= guard dropped, apply never splitting, remainders clustered
together again, the review line's summary removed. Each mutation failed
exactly the test that claims to cover it and nothing else, except "any
adjacency counts as work", which correctly failed six. =bin/lint-org=
unchanged at 0 errors / 28 warnings. Reloaded into the running Emacs and
the real pending queue rendered =writes 0:01 in 1= and =writes nothing=
on its two items.

*Corpus, 870 post-cutover guideposts in 40 spans*, pooled the way the
39.10 h baseline was taken: displayed 46.04 h (unchanged by 2(b), correct
after 2(a) -- spans now cover the long turns), written *23.05 h*, zero
zero-width spans. Raw =resume= -> =pause= pairing over the same corpus is
20.76 h, so the floor absorbs 2.29 h of sub-two-minute idle. Grouped per
session, which is how production actually clusters, written is *26.67 h*:
pooling interleaves concurrent sessions and destroys real adjacencies, so
the baseline's own grouping slightly *under*counts. Nothing in the epic's
figures was taken per session before, which is worth knowing before step
5 recomputes the accumulated record against them.

*What differed from the plan.* The plan expected 2-4 CLOCK lines per span
at a 120 s floor. Measured: median 5, p90 16, max 39. A floor sweep says
raising it does not pay -- 300 s gives median 3 and max 15 but writes
30.89 h, re-absorbing nearly 8 h of the idle 2(b) exists to stop
recording. 120 s is kept as the better trade, with the =defcustom= there
for anyone who disagrees.

*The floor does not, on its own, prevent* =>  0:00=. It merges across
short *idle*; a sub-minute turn isolated by more than the floor on both
sides survives the merge as its own run. 55 of 412 turn-pairs render as a
single minute. So =--span-work-runs= drops any run whose *rendered*
endpoints match, on the same "an interval that was never observed"
grounds the zero-width span already used. This was not anticipated during
planning and is the one place the design changed under measurement.

/Correction to the paragraph above, same day:/ "each mutation failed
exactly the test that claims to cover it and nothing else" is not what
the runs showed. Two mutations failed more than one test, not one:
"apply never splits" failed three (one-line-per-run, per-run
idempotency, and no-run-is-annotated-only -- all three genuinely depend
on it), and "any adjacency counts as work" failed six. The other five
mutations failed exactly one test each. The discriminating claim holds;
the precision claim was written from memory of the last run rather than
from the runs, which is the thing this project's own conventions warn
about.

*Step 2(c) shipped 2026-08-18*: the auto-clock-in trigger is silent by
default, behind a new =claude-code-ide-org-auto-clock-in-on-doing=
(=defcustom=, nil). That closes the last open slice of this heading.

*Reviewing the plan against the code first turned up three divergences,
and one of them changed the design.*

1. /The plan's instruction to keep the =--auto-clock-in-active= binding
   was incoherent as written./ It said the binding's second job --
   stopping the trigger destroying a pending state-change note during
   =--review-apply-state= -- "survives regardless". It does not: that
   variable's only reader is the trigger's own guard, so unregistering
   the trigger would have left a variable bound during every apply and
   read by nothing. Gating instead of unhooking is what makes the plan's
   instruction true, since a user who opts back in needs the guard
   exactly as before. This is why the change is a =defcustom= and not a
   deleted =add-hook=.

2. /The plan said =--blocker-clock-running-p= "goes quiet: nothing opens
   a clock outside apply"./ Too strong — =org-clock-in= is a user-facing
   command and nothing here takes it away, so a hand clock-in followed by
   =C-c C-t= to DONE still reaches the blocker. Corrected in that
   function's own docstring rather than in the plan, since that is where
   someone will read it.

3. /2(c) reverses a decision the cutover took deliberately./ DONE.org's
   deviation table records "retire =--trigger-auto-clock-in=" against
   "kept, and suppressed during apply via the re-entrancy guard", with
   the stated reason that a human's own =C-c C-t= should still open a
   clock. The reversal is right — a trigger-opened clock is in no queue,
   so no review pass can see or correct it, which is the second
   mechanism this heading is named for — but it costs the user that
   behaviour, and the =defcustom= exists so the cost is opt-outable
   rather than argued away.

*Verified, not just implemented:* =bin/test= 306/306. The new test
=trigger-is-silent-by-default= reads the real default rather than binding
it, which is what makes it the only test in the file that would notice a
change to that default -- every other trigger test now binds the flag to
t, since they pin the opt-in behaviour. Both mutations (default flipped
to t; gate removed from the trigger entirely) failed exactly that test
and nothing else. =bin/lint-org= unchanged at 0 errors / 28 warnings.

*Confirmed in the running Emacs, not only in batch:* after =load-file=,
=org-todo "DOING"= on a scratch heading left =org-clocking-p= nil, wrote
no CLOCK line, and still made the transition -- with the trigger still
present on =org-trigger-hook=, gated rather than unhooked.

*Corpus unchanged, as expected:* 23.70 h written over 872 guideposts
against 23.05 h over 870 before, the difference being this session's own
two new guideposts. Same 40 spans, same 255 CLOCK lines, same
distribution -- 2(c) removes a *producer* of live clocks and touches no
aggregation.

*Two things went quiet along with the trigger*, both recorded rather than
discovered later. =--clocked-heading-context= now always returns nil, so
=SessionStart= loses its "Currently clocked in" line; and the
clock-status file rests at idle between review passes. The third,
=--attention-headings-context= reporting every DOING leaf as "DOING, not
clocked", has its own heading -- see
[[id:e0904e93-b99d-44ad-b641-e3e13aced8c4][e0904e93]], which also records
why it is not a regression 2(c) introduced.

*Both decisions made 2026-08-19, and this heading closes.*

1. /The line count and the 120s idle floor are fine./ No change to
   =claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor=. Whether that value survives a
   month of data is
   [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]]'s question, not
   this one's.
2. /=C-c C-t= clocks again./ =claude-code-ide-org-auto-clock-in-on-doing=
   is back to =t=, but only after
   [[id:dadc08cf-9e12-4e93-bde8-68c241622b0f][dadc08cf]] made apply
   subtract what the trigger already recorded. So the two mechanisms
   that this heading is named for no longer disagree — they compose, and
   the double count that produced =38b92521='s 3:59-for-3:22 is
   unreachable rather than merely unlikely.

That is a better ending than the one 2(c) left. Silencing the trigger
removed the conflict by removing a mechanism; subtracting removes the
conflict and keeps both.









*** DONE The review buffer proposes spans that can only be dismissed :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-18 Tue 15:38]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       31f766ab-90ed-4950-bc5b-787528c8fe84
:CREATED:  [2026-08-17 Mon 19:06]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-18 Tue 15:33]--[2026-08-18 Tue 15:39] =>  0:06
- [2026-08-18 Tue 15:33]--[2026-08-18 Tue 15:39] assigned at review
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-18 Tue 15:34] \\
  verify both zero-width causes after 2(a)+2(b)
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-18 Tue 15:38] \\
  verified, decided, and no-op items now self-explaining
CLOCK: [2026-08-18 Tue 15:55]--[2026-08-18 Tue 16:01] =>  0:06
- [2026-08-18 Tue 15:55]--[2026-08-18 Tue 16:01] assigned at review
:END:

Three times on 2026-08-17 the review buffer offered a span reading
=[HH:MM]--[HH:MM]=, identical endpoints. Such an item cannot be assigned
usefully — an interval of zero duration records nothing — so its only
outcome is =d=. Each dismissal is a decision the human is asked to make
about a thing that carries no information.

*Two distinct causes produce the identical symptom, and they want
different fixes.*

1. *A real sub-minute span that does not cross a minute boundary.*
   Observed: =resume= at =15:20:22=, =pause= at =15:20:50= — twenty-eight
   seconds of genuine agent activity. Org timestamps are minute-precision,
   so it renders as =15:20--15:20= and is *indistinguishable from* the
   degenerate case. The span is real; only its display is lossy.

2. *The cluster is still open when the buffer is rendered.* Observed:
   =resume= at =18:51:39= with no =pause= yet when =org_pending_updates=
   was called. The current cluster held exactly one timestamp, so start
   and end were the same point. This is the more interesting defect: *the
   review buffer is offering the interval the session is still inside*,
   asking a human to assign work that has not finished.

*Not the same thing as
[[id:293ac49e-3324-421c-8495-de1a46fc04ac][293ac49e]]*, which is about
=org-clock-out-remove-zero-time-clocks= erasing short CLOCK lines org
itself wrote. This is upstream of any CLOCK line: it is about what the
review buffer *proposes*, and nothing has been written yet.

*Directions, none chosen:*

- Exclude the trailing open cluster from proposals entirely — the
  session's current span is not reviewable until it ends. Cheapest, and
  addresses cause 2 exactly.
- Suppress items below some duration floor. Addresses both symptoms but
  silently discards cause 1's real twenty-eight seconds, which is the sort
  of erasure [[id:293ac49e-3324-421c-8495-de1a46fc04ac][293ac49e]] was
  filed against. Cheap and probably wrong.
- Render sub-minute spans with seconds, so a real short span is legible as
  such and only the degenerate one looks degenerate. Fixes the ambiguity
  rather than the annoyance.

*Measured, and it is not rare.* Three in one working day, all dismissed
without comment. The cost is not the dismissal itself but what repeated
no-op items do to a review buffer: they train the eye to skim, which is
the exact failure
[[id:8e969114-0982-4c39-9258-3b25a5425e09][8e969114]] avoided by staying
silent when it has nothing to report.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/elegant-questing-lamport.md][Plan]] — the epic's plan document, which
carries this child's design rather than a file of its own.

*Verified 2026-08-18, and the plan's expectation for this heading was
wrong.* The epic plan said cause 2 "dissolves as a consequence of (a) --
verify rather than implement". It does not dissolve. 2(a) made clustering
kind-aware, which never touched the case: a trailing =resume= with no
=pause= yet still forms a cluster of one timestamp, and
=--aggregate-guideposts= closes it as =(ts . ts)=. Nothing in the
kind-aware predicate applies to a cluster with a single point in it.

Step 2(a)'s own outcome already said so -- "1 zero-width remaining (a
turn in flight, correct)" -- and that was never reconciled against this
child's premise, which had been written before 2(a) ran.

*Measured over the post-cutover corpus, per session, which is how the
review buffer actually builds items* (pooling sessions, as the epic's
other figures do, merges streams and gives the wrong answer here):
16 sessions, 57 spans.

| kind                                                    | count |
|---------------------------------------------------------+-------|
| cause 2 -- true zero-width, =start= = =end=              |     2 |
| ...of those, the session's trailing cluster              |     2 |
| ...interior, i.e. a clustering artifact                  |     0 |
| cause 1 -- real span rendering inside one minute         |     2 |
| write nothing after 2(b), total                          |     4 |
| ...that nonetheless *look* like a real interval          |     0 |

Two findings in that table. Cause 2 is now *entirely* confined to the
trailing in-flight cluster -- zero interior occurrences -- so it is a
fact about a live session rather than a clustering defect, which is a
narrower thing than the heading assumed. And the last row is a negative
result worth having: 2(b) introduced no new class of deceptive item.
Every span that writes nothing already looks degenerate; none of them
renders as a plausible interval while silently recording zero.

The two cause-1 instances are =08-11 13:16:14--13:16:25= (11s) and
=08-15 19:32:00--19:32:48= (48s), both =resume,pause= -- real turns, as
the heading said.

*Decision: explain, do not suppress.* Every no-op item now says which
kind it is, at the point of decision --
=writes nothing (a single point, not an interval)=,
=writes nothing (11s of turns, none crossing a minute)=, or
=writes nothing (no completed turn in it)=. Confirmed live in the
running Emacs on the trailing in-flight span of this very session.

Both suppression directions were rejected, with reasons:

- /Suppress below a duration floor/ discards cause 1's real seconds,
  which is exactly what [[id:293ac49e-3324-421c-8495-de1a46fc04ac][293ac49e]]
  was filed against. The heading already called this "cheap and probably
  wrong"; the measurement agrees -- it would erase 2 real turns to
  silence 2 artifacts.
- /Exclude the trailing open cluster/ needs a liveness signal, and the
  only one available is queue-file mtime with an hour's idle threshold
  (=claude-code-ide-org-queue-idle-seconds=). A crashed session's last
  span would then be invisible for an hour, which is worse than the
  annoyance. The case is self-resolving in any event: the next =pause=
  grows the cluster and the item stops being degenerate.

*What is deliberately not claimed:* the buffer still proposes items whose
only answer is =d=, 4 in 57. The recorded cost was never the keystroke,
it was that repeated no-op items train the eye to skim; an item that
states its own reason is not skimmed the same way. If that judgement
turns out wrong in use, the measurement above is the baseline to compare
against.





*** DONE Correct the accumulated record once the aggregator is fixed :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-19 Wed 08:05]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 08:15]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 08:05] \\
  re-queued, lost to my git checkout
:END:

98 post-cutover CLOCK lines carry the pipeline's error — 38 in TODO.org,
60 in DONE.org. The other 60 in DONE.org predate 2026-08-11 and need
nothing: the hooks clocked directly then, running *through* a turn and
stopping at its boundary, which is already the right method.

*Sequenced last on purpose: "corrected" is not defined until the
aggregator is.* Recomputing now with =resume= -> =pause= pairing would
bake in a method the fix might not adopt, leaving the archive carrying a
third convention — neither the original nor the final one. This runs
through whatever the fixed aggregator produces.

*The human contribution splits in two, and only one half is
recomputable.* Which heading a span belongs to is judgement exercised at
review — 36 spans carry "assigned at review" — and guideposts carry no
heading at all, so it cannot be derived. How long a span was *is*
mechanically derivable and is the part that is wrong. *Preserve every
attribution; recompute only durations.*

*The objection that no longer applies.* This was initially argued against
on the grounds that recomputation would overwrite reviewed decisions, per
[[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]], where rounding
destroyed a reviewed interval. The user withdrew that on 2026-08-18:
those ratifications were "done uncritically with imperfect recall", so
there is little judgement to protect in the durations. The b74e0f19
caution still holds for *attribution*, which is why it is preserved.

*Write the deltas before applying them* — a reviewable list of "this
heading moves from 2:52 to 1:56", not a 98-line diff. The raw evidence
survives from 2026-08-07 (910 =pause=/=resume= events, live and archived
queue files), so the recomputation is auditable against its inputs.

*Open, and the user's to settle:* what the record must be able to answer.
CLAUDE.md leaves this deliberately open — "what reports actually need to
come out the other end ... driven by concrete reporting needs, most of
which haven't been fully articulated yet". Per-heading totals make a 45%
error disqualifying; a "what was I doing Thursday" view barely notices
it. The scope of this correction should follow that answer.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/elegant-questing-lamport.md][Plan]] — the epic's plan document, which
carries this child's design rather than a file of its own.

*Applied 2026-08-18* (=6df5043=, =47cabb8=, =79f23b5=), option A: correct
what was recorded, rather than reconcile the record against everything
the queue knows. The second would have needed a human to re-attribute
about 9 hours that no guidepost carries a heading for, and the queue is
append-only, so A forecloses nothing — B stays available.

*Result.* 122 headings with a =:LOGBOOK:= scanned, 44 rewritten. The
post-cutover portion, which is what was recomputed, falls *41.32 h ->
19.95 h*. Counting all CLOCK lines including the pre-cutover ones this
never touches: TODO.org 18.17 -> 11.83 h, DONE.org 49.27 -> 36.67 h. Line
count roughly doubles, 170 -> 316, because a span written end-to-end
becomes one line per run with the idle between them no longer recorded.
Every heading attribution is preserved — the recompute rewrites intervals
inside the drawer it found them in and never moves a line between
headings.

*Three simpler versions were wrong, and each failure is the same shape:
silent, plausible, and in the direction that looks like the fix working.*

1. /Per-session pairing/ lost a background job's first turn entirely,
   because its =resume= is in the launching session's file and its
   =pause= in the job's
   ([[id:9202b39d-49e4-441c-89f2-382feb4ef1ea][9202b39d]]). 37 minutes on
   one line, which "corrected" to =0:00=. Replaced by a depth counter,
   which also gives
   [[id:7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94][7d739afd]]'s union rule for
   free.
2. /Reading only live queue files/ missed 7 archived sessions out of 18 —
   41 drawers seen against 44. Archiving takes the *oldest* sessions
   first, so what it hid was exactly the evidence behind the oldest CLOCK
   lines.
3. /Dropping only degenerate endpoints/ still wrote nine =>  0:00= lines
   into the real files, because a 20-second turn across a minute boundary
   renders =[08:14]--[08:15]= and looks ordinary. Caught by reading the
   diff, not by the suite. The files were reverted and the command re-run
   from the original record, so what landed is one clean application
   rather than two stacked.

*Verified:* no =>  0:00= line in either file; a second dry run reports 0
drawers to rewrite, so the result is a fixed point; =bin/test= 315/315
with each new test checked against a mutation of what it claims to pin;
=bin/lint-org= unchanged at 0 errors / 28 warnings.

*One test was found to verify nothing* and was rewritten. The window
extension to END + 60s was asserted by checking the line did not become
=0:00= — which passed with the extension removed, because closing an
unmatched turn at its window bound produces a perfectly plausible line.
It now asserts the =>= total, the only thing that can differ. The
docstring was corrected with it: the extension recovers under a minute
per line, worth 0.52 h across the corpus, not the rescue-from-zero the
first draft claimed. That claim was true of the superseded pairing
implementation and had been carried across unexamined — the same
plan-drift pattern this epic keeps producing.

Point the statusline (=claude-code-ide-org--statusline-task-string=)
at the *queue* files' latest event across all sessions, rather than
=org-clocking-p=/=org-clock-marker=, once those stop being live in
real time. Arguably more honest than today's version regardless of
this refactor's fate — reflects "what Claude is doing" rather than
"what's been recorded," and can't desync the way marker-based state
can, since it's just reading append-only files.

Explicit instruction, added 2026-08-06: don't stop at a bare
event-name swap. Now that a pending-queue-inspection tool
([[id:63a642c7-b04d-42dc-b6ec-3fb13df3ae04][63a642c7]]) exists in this
same plan — and, since
[[id:c9292ce8-8fb5-41c0-8de1-bbf74ffa7fce][c9292ce8]] was cancelled,
is the *only* place queue state gets surfaced outside the review
command — consider whether the statusline should surface that
same information glanceably from the terminal — e.g. a pending-review
count ("3 updates awaiting review"), or a visible distinction between
"applied" and "queued, not yet reviewed" for whatever heading it
names — rather than just relocating today's single clocked-in/out
label to a different data source with the same one-line shape.

*Third requirement, folded in from
[[id:4cda6bf7-441b-47b2-b3d5-b8c7e7a207a5][4cda6bf7]] on 2026-08-14,
which was cancelled in favour of this heading:* the label must
distinguish the *states*, not just the clock. Today's binary collapses
four distinct situations into two words — =PLANNING= and =DOING= both
read "clocked in" though one is still designing and the other
implementing; =WAIT= and =DONE= both read "clocked out" though one is
blocked and the other finished.

That heading proposed getting the keyword from =org-get-todo-state= at
the clock marker. *Do not do that here* — it is the reason it was
cancelled rather than merged as-is. The on-disk keyword lags the queue
by an entire review pass, so it would report a heading as =TODO= while
this session has already queued =DOING= on it. The keyword this
statusline should show is *the one the queue last recorded*, which is
the same source everything else in this heading reads from, and which is
also the only version that answers "what is Claude attending to" rather
than "what did the last review pass write down."

Practical consequence for whoever builds this: the five existing
=--statusline-task-string= regression tests assert on the literal
"clocked in"/"clocked out" strings and will need rewriting. Doing both
changes here means rewriting them once rather than twice.

*Promoted out of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] on
2026-08-14.* A capability the epic *enables* rather than one it
requires — the same test that promoted
[[id:5ff5a4b8-2fee-472a-b5e1-c9de8cf96704][5ff5a4b8]] and
[[id:d1cf852a-696a-4195-a4a7-039439c1f4b7][d1cf852a]] out on 2026-08-13.
Decoupling live visibility is worth doing whether or not the epic ever
closes, and the epic closes whether or not this is done.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/elegant-questing-lamport.md][Plan]] — the epic's plan document, which
carries this child's design rather than a file of its own.

*Verified, not just implemented* — shipped 2026-08-18 as step 1 of
[[id:406e9200-c3e7-49ac-a8d0-d6ff8e061ce4][406e9200]].

=--statusline-task-string= now asks the queue first, via two new helpers:
=--queue-latest-session-id= (newest queue by mtime, so one file is read
rather than every session's) and =--statusline-queue-state= (the heading
named by the newest id-bearing event, plus whether the newest guidepost is

trigger.

*A defect in the first draft, caught by the existing suite.* It returned
the empty string when the queue named a heading that would not resolve.
Wrong: an archived or deleted id is exactly that case, and the queue
having an opinion is not the same as that opinion being usable. Five
statusline tests failed — because they run against the real
=~/.claude/org-updates= and so the queue *did* have something to say. The
fix falls through to the clock when the queue id does not resolve.

*Test honesty:* three tests added, two of which fail when the change is
stashed. The third asserts the fallback and cannot discriminate, since the
old code read the clock unconditionally; it is a characterisation test and
is labelled as one.

*Not done here, and deliberately:* the second half of this heading's
original brief — surfacing pending-review state glanceably in the

=claude-code-ide-org--attention-headings-context= reports a leaf as
"DOING, not clocked" when it is not the heading =org-clock-marker= points
at. The comment beside its sibling exclusion states the standard the line
has to meet: "a report that always says the same thing stops being read,
which defeats the only purpose this one has."

It no longer meets it. The exclusion is keyed on a *live* clock, and
after [[id:226ed53b-c302-4529-870f-a42962b50bde][226ed53b]]'s step 2(c)
nothing holds one open outside a review pass, so every DOING leaf matches
every time. This session's own =SessionStart= showed two such lines and
no "Currently clocked in" line at all.

*Not a regression 2(c) introduced, which is the point worth recording.*
The 2026-08-11 cutover already stopped the MCP tools from clocking; the
trigger was the last producer, and it only ever fired on a hand-edit. So
the line has been drifting toward constant for a week and 2(c) merely
finishes the drift. Nothing to roll back — the input the exclusion reads
is simply gone.

*Not yet decided:* what the line should key on instead. The obvious
candidate is the queue rather than the marker, the same move
[[id:290b6fc5-3ebb-4827-9a97-5a3542e354b6][290b6fc5]] made for the
statusline — "has this heading seen a guidepost recently" is answerable
from the queue files and is closer to what the report is actually trying
to say. The alternative is that the line has no job left and should go.
Deciding needs a look at what a session actually wants told to it, not
just a mechanical port of the predicate.

*Decided and shipped 2026-08-21: keep the line, re-key it on file-versus-queue divergence, and retire the word "clocked".* The body left two options open -- port the predicate to the queue, or conclude the line has no job. Neither was taken as written. The queue port was right about the *source* and wrong about the *question*: "has this heading seen a guidepost recently" is still a statement about activity, and the report's actual job is to say where the file and the record disagree.

=claude-code-ide-org--attention-headings-context= now emits three kinds, backed by a new =claude-code-ide-org--queue-heading-divergence= that maps each heading :ID: to its most recent *pending* =todo= event and the timestamp of its most recent pending event of any kind:

- =DOING in file, queued -> X, not yet applied= -- the file is behind.
- =queued DOING, not yet applied= -- the queue is ahead, and the file may carry no keyword at all.
- =DOING in file, nothing queued since <ts>= -- genuinely walked away from, using the same before-today test =claude-code-ide-org-write-session-start-report= already uses, so both reports answer "first thing each day" the same way.

*It discriminates by construction*, which is the standard this heading was opened to restore: it goes silent exactly when the file and the queue agree. Pending events only -- an applied event is one the file already matches, so counting it would report a divergence at the instant it was resolved.

*Verified live against the real files and the real queue, after =load-file=* -- the running Emacs held the old definition and would otherwise have answered for it. Two lines, both true and neither constant: 420816ec as =queued DOING, not yet applied ... file says TODO=, and befaed0a as =DOING in file, queued -> DONE, not yet applied=. The two DOING containers were correctly silent. Under the old predicate the first was unreportable and the second read only "not clocked".

*Both directions were live specimens on the day, which is why both are implemented.* The old line named befaed0a, which was finished; it said nothing about c6fc6f46, which was keywordless in the file while a session actively worked it.

*Verified by mutation, not by a green suite.* Three mutations each fail the new test: deleting the file-behind branch, deleting the queue-ahead branch, and making the abandoned branch unconditional -- the last being the old always-on behaviour restored, so the test fails if the report ever goes constant again. 330/330 with the file restored.

*One boundary left deliberately unreported.* A keywordless heading whose queue holds a pending =DONE= is a real divergence and is *not* reported: by then the heading is finished as far as the record is concerned, and "the queue has unapplied items" is =org_pending_updates='s question, not this report's. c6fc6f46 was in exactly that state by the end of the session and stayed silent, correctly.

*One test changed, and the reason matters more than the wording.* =...-includes-abandoned-doing-leaf= asserted the old string; it now asserts =nothing ever queued for it= and is wrapped in =claude-code-ide-org-test--with-queue=. Without that wrapper it read the user's real =~/.claude/org-updates=, which the report's new input made load-bearing -- a suite that consults live user state can flake on it.

*** DONE An unassigned span's group heading renders an error string where a title belongs :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-18 Tue 19:47]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       98700ea3-6770-429b-84dd-1fddde60afed
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 16:00]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-18 Tue 16:00] \\
  cosmetic review-buffer rendering defect
- State "DONE"       from "TODO"       [2026-08-18 Tue 19:47] \\
  fixed, tested, confirmed live
:END:

Observed live 2026-08-18 in =org_pending_updates=, grouping two
unassigned spans:

: Error: no org heading found with :ID: "nil"  {nil}
:   ! span  - [2026-08-18 Tue 12:10]--[2026-08-18 Tue 12:12] ... UNASSIGNED

An unassigned span carries =:id nil= until a human presses =a=, which is
the normal, expected state for it — not an error. But the grouping code
resolves the id to build the group's title, and =--at-id= *returns* its
failure message as a string rather than signalling, so the message lands
where a title belongs.

*This is [[id:c2132d3f-dd78-4498-ba87-0ca814dc2f77][c2132d3f]] again by a
different route.* That heading fixed exactly this shape — an =--at-id=
error string rendered as a group heading — for an :ID: that does not
resolve, and its own note explains the mechanism: "a =cond= clause of the
form =((--at-id ...))= yields its own test, and =--at-id= returns its
error rather than signalling". The nil-id path was not covered, and it is
the one case where the missing id is *routine* rather than a defect, so
it is the case that shows up most.

Cosmetic — the spans are assignable with =a= and nothing is lost. Worth
fixing because an "Error:" in the review buffer trains the reader to
ignore errors there, which is the opposite of what that column is for.

*Filed here for findability, not because it belongs to this epic's goal.*
It was found while verifying
[[id:31f766ab-90ed-4950-bc5b-787528c8fe84][31f766ab]] and is independent
of the guidepost pipeline; refile freely.

*Fixed 2026-08-18* (=4ff1b53=). The grouping code now uses
=claude-code-ide-org--review-heading-title=, which returns *nil* when an
id does not resolve — which is what lets a =cond= tell three cases
apart: unassigned, resolvable, unresolvable. The review buffer already
had that =cond= from
[[id:c2132d3f-dd78-4498-ba87-0ca814dc2f77][c2132d3f]]; only
=org_pending_updates= was left on =--at-id=.

*The test did not discriminate on the first attempt, and the reason is
worth keeping.* It asserted =(string-match-p "unassigned -- press"
report)=, which passed with the fix mutated away — because
=string-match-p= honours =case-fold-search=, defaulting to =t=, so it
was matching the *item* line's =UNASSIGNED -- press `a'...= rather than
the group heading at all. Now binds =case-fold-search= nil and anchors
on the opening paren, which only the group line carries. Caught by
mutating the branch away, not by reading the test.

That is the second fake assertion this practice caught today — the other
was in [[id:507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e][507754ba]], where a
window-extension test asserted only that a line looked plausible.

*** TODO Concurrent sessions' turns must union, not sum, wherever time is totalled :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 16:36]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-19 Wed 08:05] \\
  re-queued, lost to my git checkout
:END:

Stated by the user 2026-08-18: two sessions running at once produce
turns that overlap in wall-clock time, and those must *union*, not sum.
=[10:00]--[10:20]= plus =[10:10]--[10:30]= is *30 minutes*, not 40 --
one stretch of elapsed time during which the agent was busy, not two
separate stretches laid end to end.

*The primitive already exists and is unwired.*
=claude-code-ide-org--merge-time-intervals= (config.el:973) sorts
=(START . END)= conses and merges anything adjacent or overlapping into
a min-to-max span -- exactly this operation. It has carried an
"UNWIRED as of 2026-08-10" note ever since. So this is a wiring
decision, not a design one, and nothing needs coining.

*Where it already bites, found while scoping
[[id:507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e][507754ba]]:* pooling
guideposts from several sessions and running
=claude-code-ide-org--span-work-runs= over the merged stream does not
merely double-count -- it *mangles* the pairing. Sorted, two overlapping
turns read =resume, resume, pause, pause=, and the run finder pairs only
the inner =resume= -> =pause=, yielding 10 minutes where the
truth is 30. Too high and too low are both reachable from the same defect,
depending on the interleaving.

The fix wherever runs are computed over multiple sessions is the same
shape: compute runs *per session*, then union the results. Never pool
the events first.

*Not the same as the overlap note in*
[[id:226ed53b-c302-4529-870f-a42962b50bde][226ed53b]], which proposes
apply refusing to write a span overlapping an existing CLOCK line on the
same heading. That is one heading's record disagreeing with itself; this
is two sessions' real, simultaneous work being counted twice.

*Open:* whether the union applies at the CLOCK-line level (never write
overlapping lines) or only at report time (clocktables and totals
de-duplicate). Those differ when two sessions genuinely work on two
*different* headings at once -- each heading's own interval is honestly
its own, and only the sum across headings overstates elapsed time. That
distinction has not been decided and should not be decided by whoever
happens to touch this first.

*Vindicated 2026-08-21, having nearly been retired.* [[id:9202b39d-49e4-441c-89f2-382feb4ef1ea][9202b39d]] proposed a depth counter over the merged stream and claimed it "gives 7d739afd's union for free, so one mechanism answers both" -- which, taken at face value, retires this heading's central instruction. It was taken at face value, in conversation, before anyone ran it.

Running it settles the matter the other way. Over the 844-guidepost corpus the depth counter returns 13.61 h against this heading's per-session-then-union at 22.79 h, because it latches: one missing =pause= leaves depth permanently above zero and no run is ever emitted again. Full numbers are in 9202b39d's body. *"Compute runs per session, then union the results. Never pool the events first" stands as written* -- pooling is exactly what fails, and it fails for the reason stated here in the first place.

*The magnitude of this heading's own concern, measured for the first time.* Across the same 10-day corpus, per-session runs sum to 22.79 h and union to 22.68 h. The double-count this heading exists to prevent is *0.11 h -- 0.5%*. Real, reproducible, and small.

That number is an argument about *where*, not *whether*. At 0.5% there is no case for paying for the union at write time, where the cost of being wrong is a CLOCK line that was refused and cannot be recovered. The report-time half is recomputable, reversible, and already written: =claude-code-ide-org--merge-time-intervals= (config.el:973) has carried an "UNWIRED as of 2026-08-10" note for eleven days and does precisely this operation.

*The open question above is deliberately still open.* Its own words -- the CLOCK-line-versus-report-time call "should not be decided by whoever happens to touch this first" -- and the ratification that would have closed it was conditional on a verification that failed. What the measurement adds is a recommendation with a number behind it, not a decision: wire =--merge-time-intervals= wherever a total is taken *across* headings, leave per-heading CLOCK lines written as computed, and let the 0.5% decide whether even that is worth doing now.

*** TODO A background job's first turn is bracketed across two session_ids :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       9202b39d-49e4-441c-89f2-382feb4ef1ea
:CREATED:  [2026-08-18 Tue 16:41]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:32] =>  0:12
- [2026-08-21 Fri 08:20]--[2026-08-21 Fri 08:32] depth-counter verification, reassigned by hand from 420816ec
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-19 Wed 08:06] \\
  re-queued, lost to my git checkout
:END:

Measured 2026-08-18 while recomputing the accumulated record for
[[id:507754ba-ae82-4f04-b209-8d8c58ac268e][507754ba]]. Today's
guideposts, grouped by =session_id=:

| session_id | resume | pause | first    | last     |
|------------+--------+-------+----------+----------|
| =0c504277= |     21 |    21 | 07:14:33 | 10:45:46 |
| =330316ca= |      1 |     0 | 10:47:22 | 10:47:22 |
| =6f28d85e= |      9 |     8 | 11:24:35 | 16:35:15 |

=330316ca= is the interactive session; its single =resume= at 10:47:22
is the =UserPromptSubmit= that *launched a background job*. =6f28d85e=
is that job, and its stream opens with the matching =pause= at 11:24:35
-- 37 minutes later, the end of the job's first turn.

So a background job's first turn has its =resume= in the launching
session's queue file and its =pause= in the job's. The two halves of one
real turn are in different files.

*What this breaks.* Any computation that pairs =resume= -> =pause=
*within* a session loses that turn entirely. In the recompute it cost
37 minutes on one CLOCK line, silently, and the line corrected to
=0:00= -- a plausible-looking answer with nothing to flag it. The fix
adopted there is a depth counter over the merged stream rather than
per-session pairing: =resume= increments, =pause= decrements, busy while
depth > 0. That also happens to give
[[id:7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94][7d739afd]]'s union for free,
so one mechanism answers both.

*This is a third explanation for
[[id:09c134c4-31eb-4f6c-aa10-f836d1d28f16][09c134c4]]'s unmatched
resumes*, and it is not the one that heading proposes. Its hypothesis is
"an interrupt or a queued follow-up" -- two =UserPromptSubmit= with no
intervening =Stop=. Here the =Stop= exists; it is simply filed elsewhere.
Worth checking how many of the 8 unmatched resumes are job launches
before instrumenting anything, since that costs nothing and this one is
already confirmed.

*Possible consequence, not verified:* CLAUDE.md says the =ExitPlanMode=
cross-session guard "comes free from the file being per-session --
another session's =PLANNING= is simply not in it to be found". If a
background job is launched by a turn that queued =PLANNING=, that event
is in the launching session's file and the job's hook reads its own, so
the promotion would not fire. Narrow -- it needs =PLANNING= set by the
very turn that launches a job -- and it has not been reproduced. Filed
as a question, not a defect.

*Verified 2026-08-21 against the raw queue corpus* -- 844 guideposts, 13 sessions, 2026-08-11 to 2026-08-21. The two halves of this heading came apart: the measurement holds exactly, the proposed mechanism does not.

*The measurement is confirmed, independently and to the second.* Reproduced straight from the =.jsonl= files: =330316ca='s lone =resume= at 10:47:22 with no =pause=, =6f28d85e='s stream opening with a =pause= at 11:24:35, gap 37 minutes. The table above is right and nothing in it needs correcting.

*The depth counter is falsified, and not marginally.* Implemented exactly as this body specifies -- merged stream, =resume= increments, =pause= decrements, busy while depth > 0 -- it produces *13.61 h* against per-session adjacency's *22.79 h*. It loses 9.18 h.

*The failure is a latch, which is why it is disqualifying rather than tunable.* Depth has no way back down once an expected =pause= never arrives. At =2026-08-17 13:09:03= depth reached 2 and never returned to 0 again for the remaining 3.8 days of the corpus, climbing to 9 and ending there. After that instant *no run is ever emitted*, because a run only closes when depth hits zero. The corpus supplies 11 unmatched =resume= -> =resume= adjacencies and 2 sessions whose stream ends on an unclosed =resume=; any one of them is enough, permanently.

*Compare the failure modes, which is the whole argument.* Per-session adjacency degrades *locally* -- a lost =pause= costs exactly one run, and the next =resume= starts clean. The depth counter degrades *unboundedly and silently* -- one lost =pause= corrupts every subsequent day. A pipeline whose stated goal is durations that mean what they say cannot adopt the second.

*Tested generously and it still fails.* Resetting the counter daily -- the framing under which the one-off =507754ba= recompute would have looked correct, since a single day's stream is usually balanced -- gives 19.95 h against 22.79 h, and errs in *both directions*: =-2.63 h= on 08-17, =+1.85 h= on 08-19. The 08-19 overcount is the one that settles it. Depth held above zero across an idle gap and re-absorbed the human's thinking time -- which is precisely the ~45% overcount [[id:406e9200-c3e7-49ac-a8d0-d6ff8e061ce4][406e9200]] exists to eliminate. The mechanism reintroduces its own parent's defect.

*The obvious repair also latches.* Keying the open set on =session_id= instead of counting makes a duplicate =resume= idempotent, so unmatched resumes stop accumulating -- but the 2 sessions that never close still pin the set open forever. 18.46 h; better, still 4.33 h short, still latching.

*So "one mechanism answers both" is withdrawn.* This heading and [[id:7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94][7d739afd]] need two mechanisms, and each is cheap on its own. That heading's "compute runs per session, then union the results, never pool the events first" is *vindicated* by the same run, not superseded.

*What survives here is a targeted linking rule, not a counter.* A session whose stream *opens* with a =pause= is a background job whose first turn began elsewhere; pair that =pause= with the nearest preceding unclosed =resume= in another session. Measured over the corpus: 3 sessions open with a =pause=, the rule fires once, recovers *0.62 h* -- exactly the 37 minutes above -- and correctly leaves the other two alone, since neither had any open =resume= to pair with.

*One caveat, recorded because the corpus could not test it.* The implementation picks the *latest* open resume when several are candidates, and every case the corpus presented had exactly one. Multi-candidate ambiguity is untested; treat the rule as measured, not as general, and re-check it the first time two sessions have unclosed resumes when a background job's pause lands.

=claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor= is a named =defcustom=. The floor
on what gets *written* is not: it is two conditions inside
=claude-code-ide-org--apply-idle-floor= — drop when the rendered
endpoints share a minute, or when the duration rounds to =0:00= — giving
an effective minimum near 30s. Shortest run actually written: 39s.

Give it a name and a default preserving today's behaviour, so both
floors have the same shape and either can be tuned without editing code.

*The value is deliberately not set here.* Measured 2026-08-19 over the
272 runs the current code writes (28.36 h), for whoever decides later:

| cutoff | lines dropped | time dropped |
|--------+---------------+--------------|
| 60s    | 25 (9.2%)     | 0.34 h (1.2%) |
| 120s   | 89 (32.7%)    | 1.84 h (6.5%) |
| 180s   | 127 (46.7%)   | 3.36 h (11.9%) |

24% of written lines currently read =>  0:01=. A 120s floor buys
symmetry with the idle floor and a one-number rule of thumb, at 6.5% of
recorded time. Decide against a month of data, not these eight days —
[[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]].

*Two mechanisms considered and rejected*, both raised 2026-08-19:
counting =[10:00]--[10:01]= as two minutes puts a =>= total on the line
that =org-clock-sum= contradicts from the same timestamps; bumping an
endpoint outward fabricates time — about an hour across the 65 lines
that would move — which is [[id:b74e0f19-5a26-4c83-9d70-8e1c5a2f6b04][b74e0f19]]'s
rounding incident pointed the other way.

*Shipped 2026-08-20 as =claude-code-ide-org-span-minimum-interval=, defaulting to 0.* Zero is the point: it reproduces today's behaviour exactly, so naming the floor moved nothing. What had been holding the line was two rendering conditions inside =claude-code-ide-org--apply-idle-floor= — endpoints inside one minute, or a duration rounding to =0:00= — which are consequences of the clock format, not a policy anyone chose. Both remain; the new variable is a third condition that does nothing until set.

*Verified by mutation, not by a green suite.* Three separate mutations each fail a distinct assertion: deleting the drop condition, defaulting it to 120, and relaxing =<= to =<==. Strictly-less matches =claude-code-ide-org-span-idle-floor=, so the two read the same way at their boundary.

*Measured for September rather than decided now.* Post-cutover corpus, 283 lines / 36.88 h: 120s drops 23.7% of lines for 3.03% of recorded time; 180s, 40.6% for 7.37%; 300s, 63.6% for 17.71%; 60s drops nothing, confirming the rendering conditions already cover that range. The sweep lives in the docstring and the value belongs to [[id:96a51c2f-ce11-4b40-bdff-11223d3d4eb7][96a51c2f]].

*The measurement that did not replicate is the more useful half.* 96a51c2f recorded 6.5% of recorded time for the same 120s value on 2026-08-18; the same measurement on 2026-08-20 gives 3.03%. No code changed — the corpus grew and the mix shifted. That heading's instruction to re-derive rather than confirm now has a two-day counterexample behind it, and its body was corrected to say so.
*** DONE The review buffer predicts a duration org does not write :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 07:52]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c6fc6f46-4954-4fdf-9d7e-7c8bf3a1a952
:CREATED:  [2026-08-20 Thu 16:43]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:28]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:29] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-20 Thu 16:28]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:29] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 16:41]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:46] =>  0:05
- [2026-08-20 Thu 16:41]--[2026-08-20 Thu 16:46] assigned at review
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-20 Thu 16:45] \\
  captured keywordless; state never set
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 07:48] \\
  one root cause, three sites; 56 written lines already self-inconsistent
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 07:52] \\
  root cause fixed at all three sites; the 56 stale lines left for a separate approval
:END:

=claude-code-ide-org--review-written-summary= sums raw seconds across a span's runs. org computes a CLOCK line's duration from the two *minute-truncated* timestamps it records. The two disagree by a minute whenever the seconds fall either side of a boundary.

Caught on a real line, 2026-08-20. The review buffer previewed =writes 0:02= for the 10:19--10:22 span; apply wrote =CLOCK: [2026-08-20 Thu 10:19]--[2026-08-20 Thu 10:22] =>  0:03=. Guideposts were 10:19:49 and 10:22:18 -- 149s, which rounds to 2, while org subtracts 10:19 from 10:22 and gets 3.

*Measured across the whole queue corpus:* of 361 runs where either method writes a minute, *25.5% disagree* -- 43 runs (11.9%) where org writes one minute less than previewed, 49 (13.6%) where it writes one more. Near-symmetric, so aggregate totals barely move; the error is per-line and does not cancel for a human reading one line.

*This is the function failing its own stated contract*, which is what makes it worth fixing rather than tolerating. Its docstring says it returns "what apply will *really* write", and the reasoning recorded there is that confirming a number which exists nowhere is worse than the overcount it replaced. A prediction that is wrong a quarter of the time reintroduces exactly that failure, one minute at a time.

*Approach:* compute the preview the way org does -- truncate each run's endpoints to the minute, then subtract -- rather than summing float seconds. That also makes the preview agree with =--apply-idle-floor='s own rendered-endpoint test, which already truncates, so the two would stop reasoning about time in different units.

*Verify:* a test pinning a run of 10:19:49--10:22:18 to a preview of =0:03=, and failing on the current =0:02=.

/Body restored 2026-08-20 by =org_amend=; the original capture silently discarded it -- see/ [[id:1cd2df62-35e9-47dc-bcce-dda7bae3c522][1cd2df62]].

*Fixed 2026-08-21, and the reported symptom was the smaller half.* Chasing the root cause found that *56 of the 353 CLOCK lines standing in TODO.org and DONE.org disagree with their own printed timestamps* -- 35 printing a minute less than their stamps imply, 21 a minute more. Same cause: raw seconds summed and rounded, where org derives a line's total by subtracting two minute-precision stamps.

*Nothing could have surfaced those 56.* A clocktable recomputes from the timestamps and never reads the =>= field, so =org_clock_report= has been quietly reporting different totals than the drawer a human was reading, with no error anywhere. The lines are *not* repaired here -- that is a data change and belongs to its own approved step.

New =claude-code-ide-org--clock-minutes= truncates both endpoints before subtracting; =--format-clock-line= derives its total from it, so a line agrees with its own stamps by construction. The preview computes *per run* rather than pooling, because org writes each line separately and summing raw seconds first also invents a minute out of combined remainders.

*Two of the three sites were live, one was not, and saying which matters.* The preview and =--format-clock-line= via the recompute caller are where the 56 came from. =claude-code-ide-org-close-open-interval= and the consolidate caller are fed endpoints already parsed from org timestamps, so raw rounding was exact there today -- routed through the helper anyway so the invariant survives either input gaining seconds. *Latent, not fixed.*

*One existing test had to be rewritten rather than re-expected, which is the more interesting finding.* =test-recompute-window-reaches-past-the-minute= pinned the =>= figure to prove the 60-second window extension mattered. Under correct arithmetic it cannot: any guidepost inside that extension truncates to the same minute as END *by construction*, so it can never move a total. Verified by removing the extension -- byte-identical output, exactly the "verified nothing" failure that test's own docstring was written to warn about. It now asserts on *membership* instead: a lone =pause= at 09:10:30 drops the line when the window includes it and leaves it whole when it does not, a difference no rounding coincidence can imitate.

*Known limit, flagged not chased.* Even corrected, the preview cannot model the already-recorded-interval subtraction apply performs at point, because an unassigned span has no heading to read a drawer from. The truncation fix removes the 25.5% class; that residual is inherent to previewing before assignment.

Mutation-tested three ways -- reverting to raw rounding, truncating only one endpoint, and removing the window extension each fail a distinct test. 329 tests.
*** DONE Fifty-six CLOCK lines disagree with their own timestamps :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-21 Fri 08:01]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       057cec53-d10e-45d0-b9ef-32713411c439
:CREATED:  [2026-08-21 Fri 07:52]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-21 Fri 07:53] \\
  data repair, awaiting approval; writer already fixed
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-21 Fri 08:00] \\
  approved; all 56 are post-cutover so the era question is moot
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-21 Fri 08:01] \\
  56 repaired, 0 remaining; all post-cutover
:END:

Measured 2026-08-21: of 353 CLOCK lines across TODO.org and DONE.org, *56 print a =>= total that does not match their own two timestamps* -- 35 a minute low, 21 a minute high. Example, TODO.org:184: =[...09:31]--[...09:36] =>  0:04=, where the stamps say five minutes.

The cause is fixed ([[id:c6fc6f46-4954-4fdf-9d7e-7c8bf3a1a952][c6fc6f46]]) and no new line can be written this way. These are the ones already on disk.

*Why nobody noticed, and why it still matters.* A clocktable recomputes from the timestamps and never reads the =>= field, so =org_clock_report= has been silently disagreeing with the drawer a human reads. Neither number is corrupt; they simply cannot both be right, and the *timestamps are the record* -- they are what org itself computes from, and what every report already uses.

*Deliberately not repaired at the same time as the code.* Rewriting the accumulated record in bulk is the shape behind [[id:79c224ab-188a-4ed4-a606-c28ee2cfe72d][79c224ab]] and the =b74e0f19= rounding incident, and the one comparable pass (the =507754ba= recompute) was explicitly approved first. Fixing the writer is safe on its own; rewriting history is a separate decision with a separate blast radius.

*Approach when approved.* A targeted totals-only rewrite: for each matching CLOCK line, recompute =>= from the two stamps already printed on it and replace *only that field*. Not a =--consolidate-logbook-text= pass, which rewrites whole drawers and is exactly the incident shape. The endpoints are never touched, so no interval changes, only the derived label agrees with the interval again.

*Verification is already written* -- the same script that found the 56 re-run after, expecting 0. Sequencing: commit first, write on disk with the buffers unmodified, =revert-buffer= without =preserve-modes=, lint, separate commit.

*Open, and the reason this is not simply a chore.* Whether pre-cutover lines should be repaired at all. They were written by the old aggregator against a different model of what an interval meant, and =claude-code-ide-org-span-minimum-interval='s docstring already treats them as a population deliberately left alone. Making their labels self-consistent is defensible; so is leaving them visibly of their era.

*Repaired 2026-08-21 on the user's approval, commit =7db9a4b=.* All 56 lines now agree with their own timestamps; the finder re-run reports 353 CLOCK lines and 0 disagreements.

*The open question above answered itself.* All 56 turned out to be *post-cutover* -- 36 in DONE.org, 20 in TODO.org -- so nothing written by the old aggregator was touched, and the judgement about whether to leave pre-cutover lines visibly of their era never had to be made. Worth recording as the reason, rather than leaving the impression it was decided.

*Verified three ways rather than one.* The script that found the 56, re-run: 0 remaining. The diff: exactly 112 lines, 56 pairs, *no pair differing anywhere before* =>= -- so no endpoint moved and no interval changed, only the derived label. And no non-CLOCK line touched at all.

*Net effect: +14 minutes across 65.65 h*, 35 lines gaining a minute and 21 losing one. Small in aggregate, which is exactly what a near-symmetric rounding error predicts -- and exactly why aggregate totals were never going to surface this. The individual lines were wrong in both directions.

*One thing the repair confirmed about the earlier count.* 365 lines in the two files carry a =>=  H:MM= string but only 353 are CLOCK entries; the other twelve are prose discussing clock lines, including this heading's own body. A whole-line anchored pattern excluded them. A looser grep would have rewritten documentation.


** DONE A hand-set TODO transition records the change but never the time :code:
CLOSED: [2026-08-19 Wed 17:47]
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       dadc08cf-9e12-4e93-bde8-68c241622b0f
:CREATED:  [2026-08-19 Wed 07:29]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 07:26]--[2026-08-19 Wed 07:37] =>  0:11
- [2026-08-19 Wed 07:26]--[2026-08-19 Wed 07:37] assigned at review
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-19 Wed 07:32] \\
  overlap-aware apply is the enabling fix
- State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 16:40] \\
  overlap-aware apply, then re-enable the trigger
- [2026-08-19 Wed 17:34]--[2026-08-19 Wed 17:34] make apply subtract already-recorded intervals
- State "DONE"       from "DOING"      [2026-08-19 Wed 17:47] \\
  overlap-aware apply shipped and verified
:END:

Raised by the user 2026-08-19: /"Don't we have to allow for the
possibility that users will toggle TODO status directly and expect it to
be accepted and incorporated gracefully?"/ Yes — and the honest answer
is that it half is.

*What already works, verified rather than assumed.* =TODO.org= declares
=DOING(d!)=, and the =!= cookie makes org write a timestamped line on
*every* entry to that state, hand-edits included. Probed live
2026-08-19 on a scratch file: =C-c C-t= to DOING leaves

: - State "DOING"      from "TODO"       [2026-08-19 Wed 07:28]

and no clock. (The probe needed =(run-hooks 'post-command-hook)= to see
it — org schedules the log line from =post-command-hook=, which does not
fire under =emacsclient -e=, so a first probe wrongly reported no line
at all.) The queue side is graceful too:
=claude-code-ide-org--review-state-stale-p= compares a queued event's
=from= against the file's *current* state, so a hand-edit that moves a
heading under a queued transition is detected and the replay refused
rather than silently applied.

So the *transition* is accepted, recorded, timestamped, and defended
against stale replay. Nothing is lost.

*What is not incorporated is time, and only in one case.* If the human
hand-sets DOING and then *Claude* works, guideposts produce a span and
the time is recorded through the queue. If the human hand-sets DOING and
works *alone*, there are no guideposts, no span, and — since
[[id:226ed53b-c302-4529-870f-a42962b50bde][226ed53b]]'s step 2(c) —
no trigger either. That work is timed only if they remember =C-c C-x
C-i=. Converting a discipline requirement back into a mechanism is
exactly what this project normally does.

*Why simply re-enabling the trigger is not the fix.* The original defect
behind 226ed53b was not "a clock exists outside the queue" — a human's
own bare CLOCK line is legitimate and expected
([[id:4f8500e6-b468-4fd4-a0f6-ebf04c7a57ba][4f8500e6]]). It was *double
counting*: =38b92521='s drawer claimed 3:59 for a session spanning 3:22,
because a trigger-opened clock and a queue-derived span covered the same
period on the same heading. That happens whenever both mechanisms are
live at once, which is precisely the case the user is asking about.

*The enabling fix is the third direction 226ed53b listed and never
took*: have apply refuse to write a run that overlaps an existing CLOCK
line on the same heading. With that in place the trigger can be switched
back on — or even defaulted back on — because the failure it caused
becomes unreachable.

Note this corrects a claim written into 226ed53b on 2026-08-18, which
called that direction moot on the grounds that "apply no longer writes a
span end to end, so there is no long interval left to overlap with".
Wrong: 2(b) makes the written lines *shorter*, not non-overlapping. A
human clocking 10:00–11:00 by hand while Claude works 10:20–10:40 still
yields 80 minutes of CLOCK for 60 minutes elapsed.

*The deeper reason it bites, worth stating once.*
[[id:b8e6007a-ae13-4b13-bb2e-a5df39435af0][b8e6007a]] separated the two
quantities *semantically* — the agenda answers "where did my attention
go", the queue answers "what was the agent doing", and the annotation
style distinguishes them. But both still write =CLOCK:= lines into the
same drawer, and =org-clock-sum= and every clocktable add them up
identically. The separation exists in the annotations and not in the
arithmetic, so any report double counts wherever the two overlap. That
is the same shape as
[[id:7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94][7d739afd]]'s union rule, one
level up: there it was two agent sessions, here it is the agent and the
human.

*Not decided:* whether apply should skip an overlapping run, trim it to
the uncovered remainder, or write it and report the overlap for a human
to resolve. Trimming is the most accurate and the most likely to
surprise; skipping is the most conservative and loses agent time the
human's clock did not really represent.

*The NEXT cascade is intact on hand-edits, and that was worth checking
rather than assuming.* Raised by the user as the special case: toggling
TODO or MAYBE to NEXT should set off side effects. It does. Probed live
2026-08-19 on a scratch file with three siblings — one already =NEXT=,
one =MAYBE=, one =TODO= — and hand-setting the =MAYBE= one to =NEXT=
produced:

: ** TODO Already the next action
:   Auto-demoted: superseded by sibling [[id:probe-maybe][Someday item]] becoming NEXT.
: ** NEXT Someday item
: ** TODO A third one

So GTD's single-next-action-per-group is enforced, the demotion is
explained in place, and the superseding sibling is named by an
=[[id:]]= link rather than a title that could later go stale.

The reason this survives is structural rather than lucky:
=claude-code-ide-org-auto-clock-in-on-doing= gates *only*
=--trigger-auto-clock-in=. =--trigger-demote-conflicting-next= and
=--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo= are registered on =org-trigger-hook=
alongside it and read no flag at all, so 2(c) narrowed exactly one
behaviour and left the cascade untouched. Confirmed by grep: the flag
has one reader in the whole file.

*Which sharpens what this heading is actually about.* Of the things a
hand-edit could reasonably expect —

| the transition is applied                    | works |
| it is timestamped in the log                 | works |
| a stale queued event cannot silently replay over it | works |
| the NEXT cascade fires                       | works |
| the work gets timed                          | *does not* |

— only the last is missing, and only when the human then works alone.
Everything else already behaves the way the user's question assumes it
should. That narrows the fix to the overlap question above rather than
to anything about how transitions are accepted.

*Shipped 2026-08-19* (=eca3a77=). Apply subtracts the heading's
already-recorded CLOCK intervals from each run before writing, so a hand
clock and a reconstructed span compose instead of summing. With that in
place =claude-code-ide-org-auto-clock-in-on-doing= is back on by
default, so a hand-set DOING times itself again.

*Subtract, not skip.* Skipping a run that overlaps would discard real
agent time falling outside the human's clock — the undercount
[[id:7d739afd-f879-445c-b0b6-65e295585a94][7d739afd]]'s rule rejects in
the other direction. A run straddling a recorded interval comes back as
the two pieces outside it.

=--logbook-has-interval-p= is removed, subsumed: a replay finds its own
previous line among the recorded intervals and subtracts to nothing. The
old check only recognised an interval written verbatim, so a re-derived
run whose endpoints moved by a second went straight past it.

*Two subtleties the tests caught, not review.* The envelope annotation
had to be gated on what was *observed* rather than what survives
subtraction — keyed on the latter a replay writes one annotation
spanning every run, a line no first pass produces. And subtraction must
only filter runs it actually cut; filtering unconditionally swallowed
the zero-width span's annotation, which reaches the writer intact
precisely so it can be annotated without a CLOCK line.

*Verified:* =bin/test= 318/318, three mutations each failing the test
that pins them. Confirmed live — =C-c C-t= to DOING opened a clock on
that heading. Note the flag needed =custom-reevaluate-setting= in the
running Emacs: =defcustom= does not reset an already-bound variable, so
the new default reaches an existing session only when set explicitly,
and otherwise at next start.

*Still open from this heading:* nothing about hand-set transitions. The
gap it named — a heading hand-set to DOING and worked alone going
untimed — is closed by the trigger being back on.



** TODO Applied review changes can be destroyed by a git checkout that looks like it reverts only Claude's edit :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       79c224ab-188a-4ed4-a606-c28ee2cfe72d
:CREATED:  [2026-08-19 Wed 08:06]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 08:02]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:08] =>  0:06
- [2026-08-19 Wed 08:02]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:08] assigned at review
- State "TODO"       from              [2026-08-19 Wed 08:07] \\
  reconciliation check is the durable fix
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 08:20]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:22] =>  0:02
- [2026-08-19 Wed 08:20]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:22] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 08:28]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:29] =>  0:01
- [2026-08-19 Wed 08:28]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:29] assigned at review
CLOCK: [2026-08-19 Wed 08:42]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:46] =>  0:04
- [2026-08-19 Wed 08:42]--[2026-08-19 Wed 08:46] assigned at review
:END:

*Incident, 2026-08-19.* Five queued transitions were applied by the
human, marked consumed in the watermark, and then destroyed —
=09c134c4= -> DONE, =507754ba= -> DONE, =7d739afd= -> TODO,
=9202b39d= -> TODO, and =507754ba= -> DOING. The queue said they were
applied; the file never carried them; =git log -S= confirms the keywords
reached no commit at all. Discovered only because someone asked whether
there were open loops.

*Cause.* Claude ran =git checkout -- TODO.org= to undo a botched edit of
its own, having first run =git status --porcelain=, seen =M TODO.org=,
and assumed the modification was entirely its own. It was not: the same
working-tree change also held the human's applied review results from
after the previous commit. One command reverted both.

*Why nothing caught it.* The watermark lives in =~/.claude/org-updates/=
and the record lives in git, so discarding the second leaves the first
claiming work that no longer exists. Nothing reconciles them, and the
review buffer reads the watermark — so the lost transitions did not
reappear as pending. They were invisible in both directions: gone from
the file, and gone from the queue's idea of what is outstanding.

This is the same class as the note already in CLAUDE.md's memory about
never running a pathspec-less =git commit=, and it generalises: *the
working tree is shared*, so any command that discards uncommitted state
— =checkout=, =restore=, =stash=, =reset --hard= — must be preceded by
reading the diff, not by assuming authorship of it.

*Recovered* by re-queueing the four transitions that mattered. The
=DOING= step of =507754ba= was not re-queued: it is now DONE, and
replaying an intermediate state would write a State line for a
transition that never happened at that time.

*Not recovered:* the =- State ...= log lines the original apply wrote,
which carried the real timestamps. The re-queued events carry today's.

*Candidate fixes, none chosen.* A reconciliation check that reports
watermark entries whose effect is absent from the file would have caught
this the moment it happened, and is the only option that catches it
whoever the culprit is. Cheaper and narrower: a hook or a documented
rule that refuses to discard =TODO.org=/=DONE.org= working-tree changes
without showing the diff first.
